Both boxes hold the one parameter, so the plain box, where it is inside or wholly past, proves what the corners would; the corners (six exact sums and two solves) are read only where it reaches an end, which keeps the instructions of `reduced_torus` and `reduced_wall` within a tenth of one per cent of the train's, counted on a development machine (reading them for every plane cost +8.96% and +12.52% in the gate). An offset whose box holds zero, an exact zero at either bound included, reaches its end, since the point may stand on that edge; one that does not lies wholly to one side, which its sign tells. Where the box reaches an end that is an edge of a domain and is off neither, one box cannot say whether the curve lies on both, and `natural` answers `None`: the pair is the engine's, as 12.7's closed forms leave any pair an enclosure cannot decide. Before, the curve was kept whole wherever the box merely reached both domains.

**The fault.** The natural fuzz target's input `082ee92f03fffe6b28ff` is a plane holding the axis of a double cone with its apex at (160, −4, 0) and slope one half, the plane 56 from the axis plus about 6·10⁻¹⁵ (δ² − 56² = +3.6·10⁻¹³ in exact rationals, since the doubles nearest 0.8 and 0.6 are not a unit vector), and the plane's domain ending at z = −112. The lower branch of the hyperbola has its vertex at z = −2δ, a hair below that edge, so no point of the branch is on the plane's domain. The edge meets the cone nowhere, as #1015's exact sign says, so no mark cuts the branch; its middle is the vertex, whose parameter on the plane is −300 less about 7·10⁻¹⁵, and the box of it holds −300. The whole branch was reported, out to z ≈ −1019. Before #1015 the engine flagged a touch on that edge, and the flag's mark happened to cut the branch. The fault itself is `trimmed`'s, from G165 and #974: it shows wherever an uncut piece's middle lies within a rounding of a domain's edge.

**Why it holds.** With no mark on it, a curve crosses no edge of either domain, so it lies on both all along or on neither (a touch of an edge is a meeting of that edge with the other surface, which is a mark). One point proven inside both, or off one, therefore decides it. A box that reaches an edge proves neither, and the closed form has no exact sign for where an arbitrary curve's point stands against an arbitrary edge, so it does not guess.

**What moves.** Only answers that were kept on a middle reaching an edge: they become the engine's. The case in `attack_g165`: a sphere of radius 2 about the origin and a cylinder of radius 1 about z, whose circles of meeting stand at ±√3, stored at h = 1.7320508075688772, √3 rounded down. A cylinder whose domain ends at h (or starts at −h) holds no point of either circle, and its end circle stands inside the sphere by 32.74's exact sign, so nothing cuts the circle at h; before, it was kept whole, a curve past the cylinder's domain all the way round. Now the pair is `None`. A domain to 1.75, or from −1.75, holds the circle with room, and it is kept as before. Likewise a torus whose tube turn runs from 1 to 2 ends at both of its equators, so the plane of its equator met it in two circles kept on middles that only reach the tube's ends; that pair is now the engine's. A middle on a whole turn's join (a cylinder or torus turn from −1 to 1) or on a sphere's pole is inside, and its piece is kept. No stored answer moves: geom-intersect's and geom-fuzz's whole suites pass, no benchmark digest moves, and the golden store's diff against the train finds no stored answer moved.

**Stretches between cuts (issue 1039).** A stretch between two cuts was still kept wherever the plain box of its middle merely met both domains. No mark lies inside a stretch either, so it too lies on both domains all along or on neither, and it is now decided as a curve no mark cuts is: kept only where its middle is proven inside both (`standing`, with a plane's corners where its box reaches an end), not kept where that box is off one, and the pair is the engine's (`None`) where it reaches an edge of a domain and is off neither. The case in `attack_g165`: the sphere of radius 2 and a cylinder of radius 1 about z whose turn runs three quarters round and whose height starts at the double above h (whose square is more than 3, read exactly), so no point of the circle at √3 is on the cylinder's domain. Its rulings meet the sphere a step below their own domain, within the step a double holds them to, so they cut the circle, and the stretch between those cuts was kept, an arc below the cylinder; now the pair is `None`, and so is its mirror below. The cost is one `standing` per stretch where `on_surface` stood, the same parameters read once more for the ends that are edges.

**What moves with it.** Pairs whose curve lies along an edge of a domain, each now the engine's: a box of a point on that edge reaches it, and the closed form has no proof that a stretch lies on the edge rather than a rounding past it. Before, each was kept on that box. In `attack_g165`'s `every_closed_form_makes_what_it_made`, pair 30: a cylinder of radius 3 on a half turn and heights from −4 to 4, and the sphere of radius 5, whose circles at ±4 lie on the cylinder's end circles. In `attack_g167`'s `every_reduction_makes_what_it_made`, pair 34: the torus of radii 3 and 1 and the tube of radius 3 from height 0 to 1, whose circle at height 1 is the tube's end circle. In `circle_part_in_plane`, the two cases of a cylinder's end circle in a plane whose edge passes through its centre (`a_centre_on_a_side_...` and `a_centre_on_a_corner_...`): the half and the quarter circle were kept, and `natural` is now `None`; `curve_surface`'s exact quarter turns there are unchanged. So a cylinder ending exactly on a plane or a sphere, its end circle the meeting, became the engine's in `natural`; issue 1044, below, brings these back with a proof.

**Along an end circle (issue 1044).** A circle whose trimming answers `None` is read once more against the end circles of each cylinder of the pair (`quadrics::along_edge`). An end circle is used only where a double holds its centre exactly (`end_centre`: on an axis along a coordinate axis the centre moves s·v along that axis, and the product and the sum each have a rounding error of zero) and where exact signs of sums of products of the stored doubles place the whole circle on the other surface (`curve_surface::circle_lies_on`): in a plane as `circle_in_plane` decides it, on a sphere by 32.74's theorem, and on a torus about its axis by (ρ − R)² + h² = r². Such an end circle lies on both whole surfaces, so it is a curve of their meeting; the closed form's circle is that end circle's where its normal is exactly parallel to the axis and the end circle's centre stands within the circle's reach of its own, and it is replaced by the end circle itself, exactly, on the closed form's frame and domain, so its parameters are the closed form's. Every point of it is on the cylinder's domain at its height exactly, so there only its turn is read (`on_turn`), and the other domain is read as before (`standing`); the trimming is otherwise the same, and a stretch whose box reaches an edge on either reading is still the engine's. No box decides that a curve lies on an edge: an end circle whose centre no double holds, or that misses the other surface by any rounding, is not used, and the pair stays the engine's. The cases in `attack_g165`: a column of radius 0.25 on a slab over [0, 10]², and a column of radius 3 on a dome of radius 5 about (1, 2, 3), built either way up, each give the whole end circle, its centre and radius the column's own; a column from 0.2 over a centre at 0.1, whose end no double holds, on a slab at 0.3 or at the double nearest 0.1 + 0.2, and a cylinder ending at the double above 4 on the sphere of radius 5, stay `None`. In `attack_g167`, a cylinder ending on a torus about (0, 0, 2) gives its end circle at 3 exactly, and one ending at 1.1 on a torus about (0, 0, 0.1) stays `None`. The four answers above come back: pin 34 of `attack_g167` and the half and quarter circle of `circle_part_in_plane` as they were before issue 1039, and pin 30 of `attack_g165` as the same two arcs on the same domains, ends and classes, now on the end circles themselves, centres ±4 where the closed form's stood at ±4.000000000000001 (the parameter boxes move with them in the last bits). The end circles are read before any trimming, for each circle of a pair with a cylinder, by a few exact sums for each end of each cylinder, which take no enclosure and cost the meter nothing. Where the closed form's circle is the end circle to the bit (its centre's and radius's doubles the end circle's, on its own frame and domain), it is trimmed on its turn alone (`quadrics::trimmed_or_along`): every point of it is at the end's height exactly, so a reading on both whole domains either agrees with the reading on the turn or reaches an edge and stops that trimming, and the one trimming keeps what the two in turn keep. So the `natural` fuzz target's stored input of a column of radius 5 across the edge of a slab (32.69) gives the half of its end circle the slab holds at 70 units, its work before issue 1039, where trimming on both whole domains first took 75 and passed its pin in `what_the_closed_form_leaves_costs_no_more_than_today`. Any other circle is trimmed on both whole domains first, and along an end circle only where that answers `None`, at one more trimming's work. Cones' end circles, whose radius r₀ + k·v is another rounded sum, and end circles on other kinds, stay the engine's.

**Not changed.** A revolution's or extrusion's parameters are the box of its whole domain by construction (`quadrics::parameters`), so no end of theirs is an edge here.

**Started from:** none. A fix to the kernel's own `trimmed` (G165); no other code was read for it.

**Changes:** this record; 12.7's paragraph on trimming; `geom-intersect`'s `quadrics.rs` (`Standing`, `part_turn` and `short_of_pole`, which `edges` and `rims` share, `rims`, `on_plane`, `standing`, and `on_both`, which reads both domains' standing for a curve no mark cuts and for each stretch between cuts; `trimmed` and `items_of` may answer `None`, and `natural` passes it on) and its header; `tests/attack_g165.rs` (`a_stretch_between_cuts_is_kept_only_where_its_middle_is_proven_inside` and pin 30 of `every_closed_form_makes_what_it_made` for issue 1039, with pin 34 of `tests/attack_g167.rs`'s `every_reduction_makes_what_it_made` and `tests/circle_part_in_plane.rs`'s `end_circle_domains`, `a_piece_no_edge_cuts_is_kept_only_where_its_middle_is_proven_inside`, `a_piece_whose_middle_lies_on_a_join_or_a_pole_is_kept`, `a_piece_no_edge_cuts_is_nothing_off_a_domain_and_the_engines_at_its_edge`, `a_box_that_ends_exactly_at_an_edge_reaches_it`, `a_far_plane_reads_a_piece_past_an_end_from_its_corner`, `an_offset_from_a_corner_that_ends_at_zero_reaches_it`); `geom-fuzz`'s `natural` target, the issue's input stored in `crashes`. For issue 1044: `quadrics.rs` (`placed` and `both`, which `standing` and `on_both` share, `on_turn`, `Along`, `along_edge`, `end_centre`, `within`; `trimmed` and `on_both` take `along`, and `items_of` reads a piece along an end circle through `trimmed_or_along`, where `along_edge` also says whether the piece is that end circle to the bit) and its header; `curve_surface.rs`'s `circle_lies_on`; `tests/attack_g165.rs` (`a_column_on_a_slab_or_a_dome_meets_it_in_its_end_circle`, with the column across a slab's edge and its work, `a_cylinder_ending_a_rounding_off_a_surface_is_the_engines`, pin 30) and `tests/attack_g167.rs` (`a_cylinder_ending_on_a_torus_meets_it_in_its_end_circle`, pin 34); `tests/circle_part_in_plane.rs`'s `end_circle_domains` and its two cases, back as before issue 1039.

## 32.101 A cylinder's silhouette whose domain ends on a ruling is that ruling and its half-turns, decided exactly

**Decision (the level fuzz target's three stored timeouts).** `trace` asks the function for a closed form first (`Level::closed`, none by default). `Silhouette` has one on a cylinder whose domain in u ends on a ruling of the silhouette: the answer is every ruling in the domain, each one branch across v from end to end, its two vertices exact, ends `Boundary`, deviation 0, nothing flagged, for one unit of work. Every other case goes to the search as before, with the same answer and the same count: a cylinder whose rulings all lie inside the domain, one whose frame's unit vectors x̂ and ŷ are not doubles exactly, a direction with a factor too small or too large for exact products, a view along the axis (g vanishes everywhere), and every other kind and function.

| Input | Shape | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| the level target's stored timeouts `7f20fe1f` and `dbaa306c` (14 November), and `1f6be9ab` (Phase 6's run on the Phase 5 train, pull request 866) | a cylinder of radius 4.5, 1.5 and 1 kept from 1/16 to 1/4 of a half-turn, seen along (4, −4, −4), whose silhouette is the ruling u = 1/4, the domain's end | 1,048,577 units each, stopped by the target's budget of 2^20 (1.6 to 2.0 s a trace, optimised, on a development machine), and 16,777,217 at 2^24 (26 to 32 s), still stopped | that ruling, 1 unit |

**Why the search could not.** On a cylinder c + r·e(u) + s·v·n̂ the normal is a positive multiple of s·e(u), so g = N·d is a nonzero multiple of a·cos πu + b·sin πu, a = x̂·d and b = ŷ·d, the same all along v. Here a + b = 0, so g is exactly zero along the whole side u = 1/4. No enclosure can sign it there, and its slope along the side is zero, so `roots` on that side split it to the floor (a side of length 1 at a floor of 10⁻⁵), in every box along it, at every level of the boxes' subdivision. No budget the target can afford reaches the end.

**Why it is exact.** The zero set of a·cos πu + b·sin πu, with a and b not both zero, is a ruling u* and every u* + k, k whole. A domain end is a double, so rational. Where x̂ and ŷ are doubles exactly (each enclosure `Frame::unit_vectors` gives is one point) and every factor is zero or between 10⁻⁷⁵ and 10⁷⁵ in size (`tame`, as in 32.85), a, b, a + b and a − b are sums of products of doubles whose signs `curve::sign_of` gives exactly. At a zero, tan πu is rational or infinite; by Niven's theorem (I. Niven, *Irrational Numbers*, 1956, corollary 3.12) a rational multiple of π with a rational tangent has tangent 0 or ±1, so the end is a whole number of quarters, where cos πu and sin πu are 0, ±1 or ±√2/2, and the end is a ruling exactly when a (whole half-turns), b (half-turns and a half), a + b (a quarter or five quarters past them) or a − b (three or seven quarters) is zero (`vanishes`). An end of size past 2^50 is not read, so that each end + k for k up to 2 in size is exact; the domain is at most a turn wide, so those are all its rulings. A ruling on both ends of a closing domain is one ruling, kept at the first. No tolerance and no rounded comparison is used.

**What stays the search's**, at its old cost: a domain end a step from a ruling but not on it (an enclosure cannot sign g there either, so the search still runs to its budget; no input has shown it), the same ruling on a frame whose unit vectors are not doubles, and a level function other than `Silhouette` whose zero set runs along a domain end (an isocline or an envelope on a cylinder can; none has been found).

**Started from:** none found. Searched tangency-labs/solidcore at 72686a8c3a961ddd7112914b9d0ac5e835c053bb by code search (silhouette): `intersect/surface_silhouette.rs` traces a silhouette on any surface with SMLib's tolerance-driven tracer from boundary solutions, and has no closed form for a cylinder.

**Changes:** this record; chapter 12.8's as-built line for G168; `geom-intersect`'s `level.rs` (`Level::closed`, `Silhouette`'s, `rulings`, `vanishes`, `tame`) and its header; `tests/silhouette_rulings.rs`; `geom-fuzz`'s `level` target (`answer_of`, the new input stored in its `crashes` folder and all three held to their answer and count) and `piercing` target (the timeout of the same run, a segment lying in an off-grid plane that 32.85 answers in one unit, stored and held with the other seven).
